अध्याय 6

माप लेना: Body Measurement से Standard Chart तक

माप लेना: Body Measurement से Standard Chart तक

माप में accuracy = perfect fit = satisfied customer

📌 अध्याय का उद्देश्य
Body measurement techniques, Standard Size Chart development, Ease allowance, Tolerance और fitting problems का systematic solution।

6.1 — माप का महत्व: Tailoring और Manufacturing में

"नाप ग़लत तो सब बेकार।"

Garment का सबसे पहला foundation माप है। चाहे आप एक individual customer के लिए tailor कर रहे हों या 10,000 shirts manufacture कर रहे हों — माप की accuracy fundamental है।

Tailoring में: Customer की exact body measurements लेकर उनके लिए specific garment बनाते हैं। Fit personal होता है। हर measurement unique होती है।

Manufacturing में: Standard size chart use होती है। एक standard size के pattern को mass में cut और sew किया जाता है। Fit "best fit for that size" होता है — exact personal fit नहीं।

दोनों में common:

Consistency: हर बार same measurement same way लें।

Accuracy: ±1-2 mm accuracy target करें।

Documentation: Measurements record करें।

Standard Tools: Flexible tape measure, right technique।

Measurement errors के consequences:

Tailoring में: Poor fit, unhappy customer, alteration cost, reputation damage।

Manufacturing में: Rejected lot (if out of tolerance), returns, compliance issues with buyer।

Measurement एक science है — intuition नहीं। Proper technique, consistent practice, and regular verification से accurate measurements आती हैं।

6.2 — Body Measurement: Complete Guide

Body measurements को systematically लेना एक skill है। इसे practice से develop करते हैं।

Basic Rules:

Tape measure: Flexible, non-stretch 150 cm tape। Steel tape use मत करें।

Posture: व्यक्ति normal posture में खड़ा हो। Slouching नहीं।

Breathing: Normal breathing। Take measurement as person breathes normally।

Tape position: Horizontal और level जहाँ required।

Ease: Body measurement पहले, ease बाद में add करें।

पुरुषों की Body Measurements:

CHEST (Seena):

How: Tape को armhole के ठीक नीचे, सबसे चौड़े हिस्से पर। Arms थोड़े ऊपर करके tape place करें, फिर नीचे करें।

Note: Slightly relaxed — too tight नहीं, too loose नहीं।

Standard Ease: Shirt के लिए +5-6 inches।

WAIST (Kamar):

How: Natural waist line पर — जो ribs और hips के बीच में सबसे narrow जगह है।

Note: Breathe out करने पर measure करें।

SEAT/HIP (Kamanee):

How: सबसे चौड़ी जगह पर — generally crotch से 7-9 inches ऊपर।

SHOULDER WIDTH:

How: Back पर — एक shoulder point से दूसरे तक। Spine के natural curve को follow करते हुए।

BACK LENGTH:

How: Nape of neck (collar का center back) से waist तक।

NECK:

How: Collar bone के level पर।

Note: +1 inch ease for shirt collar।

SLEEVE LENGTH:

How: Shoulder point से wrist bone तक। Arm slightly bent।

TROUSER INSEAM:

How: Crotch से ankle तक — inner leg पर।

TROUSER OUTSEAM:

How: Natural waist से ankle तक — outer leg।

THIGH CIRCUMFERENCE:

How: Fullest part of thigh।

TROUSER RISE:

How: Natural waist से crotch — front और back दोनों।

महिलाओं की Body Measurements (additional):

BUST:

Sew across fullest part of bust — bra line level। Arms down।

OVER BUST (High Bust):

Just above the bust — under arms level।

UNDER BUST:

Just below the bust।

HIP:

Fullest part — approximately 8-10 inches below natural waist।

FRONT LENGTH (Shirt/Blouse):

Shoulder to desired length — front।

BACK LENGTH:

Nape to desired length — back।

ARMHOLE DEPTH:

Back neck to armhole — back।

Children's Measurements:

Children के लिए height-based sizing common है। Standard charts available हैं। Regular measurements important — children grow fast।

6.3 — Standard Size Chart: Manufacturing का आधार

Standard Size Chart garment manufacturing की backbone है। एक बार develop करें — बार-बार use करें।

Indian Men's Shirt Size Chart (Indicative Example — actual standards vary):

Size | Chest | Collar | Shoulder | Sleeve | Back Length

XS | 36" | 14" | 16.5" | 24.5" | 27"

S | 38" | 14.5" | 17" | 25" | 27.5"

M | 40" | 15" | 17.5" | 25.5" | 28"

L | 42" | 15.5" | 18" | 26" | 28.5"

XL | 44" | 16" | 18.5" | 26.5" | 29"

XXL | 46" | 16.5" | 19" | 27" | 29.5"

3XL | 48" | 17" | 19.5" | 27.5" | 30"

⚠️ यह example/indicative chart है। Actual standards buyer specification या brand standards के अनुसार होती हैं।

Size Chart Development Process:

Step 1: Target market identify करें। School students? Corporate employees? Retail customers?

Step 2: Survey करें। Minimum 50-100 people के measurements। Excel में record करें।

Step 3: Data analysis करें। Average, range, distribution find करें।

Step 4: Size breaks decide करें। Typically 2 inch grade per size।

Step 5: Fit model पर test करें। Each size के liye appropriate fit model।

Step 6: Refine करें। Feedback के आधार पर।

Step 7: Document और freeze करें। This becomes your standard.

International Size Differences:

Indian sizes generally larger than European। US sizes different from EU। Children's sizes may be by age or height। Export करते समय buyer's size chart follow करें — never assume।

6.4 — Ease Allowance: Fit और Function दोनों

Ease एक critical concept है जिसे बहुत से लोग ignore करते हैं।

Body Measurement = आपके शरीर का actual size।

Pattern Measurement = Body Measurement + Ease।

Ease क्यों ज़रूरी है:

Movement: Sitting, standing, reaching — all require ease। No ease = cannot move।

Comfort: Tight garment = uncomfortable।

Aesthetic: Every garment has a desired silhouette — loose, fitted, oversized।

Ease के Types:

Wearing Ease (Functional Ease): Movement के लिए minimum ज़रूरी।

Standard Wearing Ease:

Chest (Shirt): 4-6 inches

Chest (Jacket): 3-4 inches

Waist (Trouser): 1 inch

Hip (Trouser/Skirt): 1-2 inches

Shoulder (Shirt): 1/2 inch each side

Design Ease (Aesthetic Ease): Desired silhouette के लिए extra।

Fitted shirt: Wearing ease only (+4 inches on chest)

Semi-fitted: +6 inches

Regular fit: +6-8 inches

Loose/Relaxed: +8-12 inches

Oversized: +12 inches+

Negative Ease: Knit fabrics में possible। Garment smaller than body — fabric stretches to fit।

Example: Fitted T-shirt — 2 inches smaller than chest। Fabric stretch accommodates।

Ease और Fabric की Relationship:

Woven fabric (no stretch): More ease ज़रूरी।

Knit fabric (stretch): Less ease, sometimes negative।

2-way stretch knit: Significantly less ease।

4-way stretch (lycra): Smallest ease।

Practical Examples:

Formal shirt (woven): Chest ease = 5-6 inches

T-shirt (single jersey knit): Chest ease = 2-3 inches (or slightly negative)

Swim trunks (stretch woven): Slight negative ease।

6.5 — Tolerance और Industrial Measurement

Industrial Garment Manufacturing में "perfect" measurement exist नहीं करती। हर piece थोड़ा different होगा। Question है — कितना different acceptable है?

Tolerance: Allowed variation in measurement। यह range define करती है — इसके अंदर = PASS। बाहर = FAIL।

Tolerance का importance:

Human operators machines नहीं हैं। हर cut slightly different। हर stitch different। Tolerance = realistic standard।

Standard Garment Tolerances (General Guide — buyer specifications vary):

Measurement Point | Tolerance

Chest | ±1 cm

Length | ±1 cm

Sleeve Length | ±0.5 cm

Collar | ±0.5 cm

Shoulder Width | ±0.5 cm

Pocket Size | ±0.5 cm

Pocket Position | ±0.5 cm

Width measurements | ±0.5-1 cm

⚠️ Actual tolerances buyer/brand specification से determine होती हैं। Export buyers generally strict tolerances specify करते हैं।

Measurement Taking in QC:

Garment को flat, relaxed state में रखें — no tension।

Seam-to-seam measure करें।

Right way to measure specific points — buyer's measurement guide follow करें।

Document करें — deviation noted।

AQL-based measurement inspection:

Lot size → Sample size (AQL table से)।

Random sample pick करें।

Measure specified points।

Defects count करें।

Accept/Reject decision।

6.6 — Fitting: Theory से Practice तक

Fitting वह process है जहाँ theory (pattern) और reality (human body) मिलते हैं।

Fitting Types in Industry:

Individual Fitting (Tailoring): Customer पर directly।

Fit Model Fitting: Standard body size के person पर।

Dress Form Fitting: Mannequin पर। Less accurate for woven than knit।

Virtual Fitting (3D): Software से — Browzwear, CLO3D। Future of sampling।

Common Fitting Problems और Solutions:

Problem 1: Chest Too Tight

Solution: Side seams release करें। Dart width adjust करें।

Problem 2: Back Pulling / Horizontal Fold

Solution: Back seam add करें या back length increase।

Problem 3: Shoulder Width Wrong

Solution: Shoulder seam re-sew। Armhole reshape।

Problem 4: Sleeve Too Long/Short

Solution: Sleeve length alter। Usually at cuff।

Problem 5: Collar Too High/Low

Solution: Neckline raise or lower। Pattern adjust।

Problem 6: Seat Too Tight

Solution: Back crotch curve adjust। Seat seam release।

Problem 7: Trouser Front Crotch Pull

Solution: Front rise increase। Crotch curve adjust।

PP Sample Fitting Process (Industrial):

PP Sample = Pre-Production Sample।

Pattern maker creates sample → Factory produces garment → Fit session with fit model → Issues noted → Pattern corrections → Revised sample → Buyer approval → Production starts.

This process ensures problems are caught before bulk production. Every correction at PP stage saves much more in production stage.

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

विस्तृत अध्ययन: Measurement — Beyond the Tape

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Measurement in garment industry goes beyond just taking body measurements. It encompasses a complete system of standards, tolerances and verification.

Grading और Size Systems — International Comparison:

India में standard sizes का कोई single universal system नहीं है। Different brands, different standards।

International Size Systems:

US: XS=34, S=36, M=38, L=40, XL=42, XXL=44 (chest in inches)।

UK: Same as US generally।

EU: 44, 46, 48, 50, 52, 54 (chest in cm approximately)।

China: S=165/84A, M=170/88A (height/chest)।

India: Varies by brand — always check specific brand chart।

For export: Always use BUYER'S size chart। Never assume।

Measurement Tape and Tools:

Flexible tape measure: 150 cm (60 inch)। Must be non-stretch। Check accuracy periodically against steel rule।

L-Square: For right angles। Shoulder measurement, right angle seams।

French Curve: For armhole, neckline, hip curves।

Hip Curve Ruler: Specifically for side seams, hip lines।

Pattern notcher: V-notch in pattern। Critical for alignment।

Grading ruler: Specialized for grade increments।

Body Scanning Technology: 3D body scanners now available। Used by some brands for size development। Very accurate। Not common in Indian small factories yet।

Measurement Documentation System:

For each style, create Measurement Specification Sheet:

Style: _____ | Season: _____ | Date: _____

MEASUREMENT POINT | HOW TO MEASURE | XS | S | M | L | XL | TOL+/- |

1/2 Chest | 1" below armhole, flat | | | | | | ±1cm |

Total Length (CB) | Neckpoint to hem | | | | | | ±1cm |

Shoulder width | Seam to seam (back) | | | | | | ±0.5cm |

Sleeve length | Shoulder pt to cuff edge | | | | | | ±0.5cm |

Cuff opening | Around cuff, buttoned | | | | | | ±0.5cm |

Collar stand height | Stand height at CB | | | | | | ±0.3cm |

Collar length | Collar tip to tip | | | | | | ±0.5cm |

Pocket width | Opening width | | | | | | ±0.3cm |

Pocket height | Top to bottom | | | | | | ±0.3cm |

Pocket placement | Center front to pocket edge | | | | | | ±0.5cm |

"How to Measure" column is critical — standardizes measurement technique।

QC Measurement Protocol:

During inline inspection:

• Measure first piece of every new size।

• If within tolerance: Continue।

• If out of tolerance: STOP। Investigate immediately।

During final inspection:

• Measure as per AQL sample plan।

• All critical measurements (chest, length, sleeve) must pass।

• Document all measurements।

Alteration और Rework:

When garment is out of tolerance, options:

• Within 1-2 cm over: Can reduce (let out seam, trim hem)।

• Within 1-2 cm under: More difficult (let out seam if allowance exists)।

• Major deviation: Rework or scrap।

Rework cost: Double the operation cost plus QC re-inspection।

Prevention always better than rework।

Pattern Grading — Advanced:

Grade rules are developed from body measurement surveys।

Indian Grade Rules (Indicative — verify with actual market research):

Men's Shirt — Chest Grade (S to M to L to XL):

Chest: +2 cm per size (0.5 cm each side × 4)।

Length: +1 cm per size।

Shoulder: +1 cm per size (0.5 each side)।

Sleeve length: +0.5 cm per size।

Collar: +0.5 cm per size।

Women's Dress — Bust Grade:

Bust: +4 cm per size (1 cm each side × 4)।

Waist: +3 cm per size।

Hip: +4 cm per size।

Length: +1.5 cm per size।

Shoulder: +0.5 cm per size।

These rules are applied at every key point on every pattern piece। Computer grading: Software applies rules simultaneously to all points। Manual grading: Done point by point with ruler।

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

✅ अध्याय सारांश
Body measurement systematic technique से लें। Standard Size Chart — market research पर based, documented और standardised। Ease = wearing ease + design ease। Tolerance = acceptable variation range। PP Sample fitting — production से पहले सभी issues fix करें।

अध्याय 7

Pattern Making: Basic Pattern से Industrial Grading तक

Pattern Making: Basic Pattern से Industrial Grading तक

Pattern — वह mathematical blueprint जिससे identical garments बनते हैं

📌 अध्याय का उद्देश्य
Pattern making की complete methodology, grading process और marker making efficiency।

7.1 — Pattern Making: Science और Art दोनों

Pattern Making एक ऐसी skill है जो engineering precision और aesthetic sensibility दोनों demand करती है।

Pattern क्या है: एक 2D template जो garment के हर piece का shape और size define करता है। इसी template को fabric पर रखकर cutting होती है।

Pattern Making के Types:

1. Flat Pattern Making: Measurements से paper पर directly pattern draft करना। सबसे common industrial method। Mathematical और systematic।

2. Draping (Moulage): Fabric को dress form पर directly shape देना। 3D से 2D में आना। Haute couture और creative design में।

3. Computer-Aided Pattern Making: Software (Gerber, Lectra, Optitex) से। Faster, more accurate। Large factories में standard।

Pattern Making क्यों critical है:

Pattern गलत = सब गलत। एक 5 mm की error pattern में → हर piece में वही error → bulk production में = बड़ा problem।

Pattern accuracy investment:

अच्छे pattern maker को adequate time दें। Pattern को test करें (toile)। Documentation करें। Archive करें।

Pattern Making Tools:

Hip curve ruler, French curve, L-square, Straight ruler, Tracing wheel, Pattern paper, Pencil, Notch maker।

7.2 — Basic Block (Sloper): Foundation Pattern

Basic Block (Sloper) = Foundation pattern जो exact body measurements पर बनाया जाता है — बिना design ease के।

Men's Shirt Basic Blocks:

1. Front Body Block: Front panel। Center front line, shoulder slope, armhole, side seam, hem।

2. Back Body Block: Back panel। Center back, yoke, armhole, side seam।

3. Sleeve Block: Full sleeve। Crown, underarm, cuff।

4. Collar Block: Collar pattern। Stand height, collar width, points।

5. Cuff Block: Cuff pattern।

Front Body Block Draft करना (Basic Steps):

Materials: Pattern paper, pencil, ruler, French curve।

Step 1: Center Front Line draw करें। Length = front length।

Step 2: Chest Line draw करें — perpendicular। Width = (Chest ÷ 4) + ease।

Step 3: Waist Line: Front length के proportion पर।

Step 4: Shoulder: Angle और length।

Step 5: Armhole: French curve से।

Step 6: Neck: French curve से — नीचे और across।

Step 7: Side seam, hem।

Step 8: Mark notches — armhole, waist।

Block Testing:

Paper pattern से test garment बनाएँ — cheap calico fabric में। Fit model पर check करें। Issues note करें। Pattern revise करें। फिर test। Repeat until perfect।

Block Archiving:

Manila paper पर draw करें — durable। Date, size, revision number clearly mark करें। Store flat — fold नहीं करें। Digital backup — photo या scanner।

7.3 — Style Development: Block से Production Pattern तक

Basic Block = Starting point। Style Pattern = Production ready।

Style development process:

Step 1: Design Sketch से measurements और style details note करें।

Step 2: Block select करें जो closest है।

Step 3: Design ease add करें।

Step 4: Style lines add करें — pocket position, yoke line, pleats, darts।

Step 5: Style-specific details:

- Collar: Convert block to style collar

- Placket: Add button stand, button hole position

- Pocket: Size, position

- Cuff: Style, width

Step 6: Seam Allowance add करें।

Seam Allowance Standards:

Neck seam: 0.7-1 cm।

Side seam: 1-1.5 cm।

Shoulder seam: 1 cm।

Sleeve seam: 1 cm।

Hem: 2-3 cm।

Cuff: 1 cm।

Notches और Markings:

Notches: Seams को align करने में help। V-notch या slit।

Drill Holes: Pocket corners, dart ends, button positions।

Grain Lines: Each piece पर clearly marked।

Fold Lines: Center front, center back।

Size Label: Pattern piece पर clearly।

Pattern Documentation:

Style Number, Date, Size, Piece Name, Grain Line, Notch positions, Total pieces in style।

Pattern Quality Check:

Seam lengths match करते हैं? Curves smooth हैं? Notches aligned हैं? Seam allowance consistent है?

7.4 — Grading: एक Pattern से सभी Sizes

Grading = एक base size के pattern से सभी required sizes generate करना।

Grading का Logic:

हर size पर body measurements एक fixed amount से बढ़ती हैं। इसे Grade Rule कहते हैं।

Typical Grade Rules (Indicative — actual varies by buyer/style):

Men's Shirt, per size change (S to M, M to L, etc.):

Chest: +2 cm (0.5 cm each side × 4 seam)

Length: +1 cm

Shoulder: +0.5 cm each side

Sleeve: +0.5 cm length

Collar: +0.5 cm per size

Women's Dress, per size change:

Bust: +4 cm

Waist: +3 cm

Hip: +4 cm

Length: +1.5 cm

Grading Points (x,y coordinates):

हर pattern piece पर key points होते हैं जो grading में move होते हैं।

Example for chest:

Point: Side seam, chest level।

Move: X = +0.5 cm (outward), Y = 0।

Manual Grading:

Base size pattern लें। Each grade point को rule के according shift करें। New size trace करें। Seams और curves smooth करें।

Computer Grading:

Software में base size import करें। Grade rules define करें। Software automatically सभी sizes generate करता है। Minutes में complete।

Grading Nest:

सभी sizes को एक sheet पर overlay करके देखना। Graduation uniform है? Curves consistent? Proportions maintained?

Common Grading Mistakes:

Grade points wrong → sizes distorted।

Curves not smoothed → production problems।

Notches not graded → alignment issues।

7.5 — Marker Making: Fabric Efficiency का Science

Marker = सभी pattern pieces को fabric width पर arrange करना ताकि waste minimum हो।

Marker Efficiency:

Marker Efficiency % = (Total Pattern Area / Total Marker Area) × 100

उदाहरण:

100 shirts के pattern pieces: Total area = 150 square meters

Marker area (fabric used): 170 square meters

Efficiency = 150/170 × 100 = 88.2%

Target Efficiency:

Woven fabric: 85-92%

Knit fabric: 80-88%

Complex patterns (checks, plaids): 75-85%

Marker Making Rules:

1. Grain Line: Pattern piece का grain line fabric grain से match होना चाहिए। Crosswise grain से off हो तो garment twist।

2. Nap Direction: Velvet, corduroy, suede, brushed fabric — हर piece same direction। Otherwise shade variation।

3. Pattern Matching: Checks, stripes, prints — patterns align होने चाहिए। Extra fabric consumption होती है।

4. Face Up/Face Down: Fabric face side कौन सी है — pattern accordingly।

5. Symmetrical vs Asymmetrical pieces।

Marker Layout Techniques:

Manual Marker: Pattern pieces को paper पर arrange करें, trace करें।

Computer Marker (Automatic Nesting): Software automatically optimizes। Typical improvement: 2-5% over manual.

Marker for different order quantities:

Single size marker: सभी pieces one size।

Multi-size marker: Different sizes mixed — more efficient for mixed size orders।

Practical Marker Making:

1. All pattern pieces list करें — how many of each।

2. Fabric width note करें।

3. Any restrictions (nap, pattern match) note करें।

4. Arrangement करें — large pieces first, gaps fill करें with smaller pieces।

5. Efficiency calculate करें।

6. Approve करें before cutting।

Marker Optimization Tips:

Small pieces (cuffs, collars) को large pieces के gaps में fill करें।

Symmetrical pieces को flip करके place करें।

Collar pieces को inverted place करें between body pieces।

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

विस्तृत अध्ययन: Pattern Making — Industrial Standard

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Industrial Pattern Making में precision, documentation और repeatability सबसे important हैं।

Pattern Documentation System:

हर pattern piece पर ये marks होने चाहिए:

• Style number / Style name।

• Season (if applicable)।

• Size।

• Piece name (Front Body, Back Body, Collar, etc.)।

• Grain line — long straight arrow।

• Fold line (if any) — double arrow।

• Notches — position and type।

• Drill holes — position।

• Date created।

• Version number (V1, V2...)।

• Total number of pieces in style।

"Cut 1", "Cut 2 pairs", "Cut on fold" — clearly marked।

Pattern Storage:

Short-term (current season):

Manila paper patterns — flat storage। Cardboard tubes for rolled patterns।

Long-term archive:

Digital scan of every pattern piece। PDF format, high resolution।

Physical: Labeled envelopes, year-wise।

Pattern retrieval: Must be able to find any pattern within 5 minutes।

Computer Pattern Making — Deep Dive:

Modern garment factories use CAD for pattern making। Key software:

Gerber Accumark:

Industry standard for large factories।

Features: Pattern design, grading, marker making, 3D simulation।

Price: Expensive — ₹2-5 Lakh+ for full package।

Used by: Large export factories, established brands।

TUKAcad:

More affordable option।

Good features for medium factories।

Free trial available।

Windows-based।

Optitex:

Strong 3D simulation।

Good for fashion industry।

Browzwear / CLO3D:

Best 3D visualization।

Digital sampling।

Growing adoption among brands।

Sustainable Pattern Making:

In modern industry, sustainability is becoming important।

Pattern making decisions affect:

• Fabric waste (marker efficiency)।

• Fabric type (some fabrics generate more waste in certain directions)।

• Season length (patterns designed to work across multiple seasons)।

Zero-waste pattern making: Design garments where offcuts are used in the same garment or coordinating pieces। Growing trend in sustainable fashion।

Pattern Making for Different Fabric Types:

Woven fabric (no stretch):

• Standard seam allowances।

• Grain line critical।

• Pattern pieces don't stretch।

Stretch woven (2-way stretch):

• Reduce ease in stretch direction।

• Seam allowance consideration (may use serged seam)।

Knit fabric:

• Significant ease reduction (may be negative ease)।

• Consider recovery (how much it springs back)।

• Different pattern shapes at armhole, neckline।

Asymmetric patterns (non-reversible):

• Must specify face up/face down।

• Extra pieces may be needed।

• Cannot flip pattern pieces।

Checks and stripes:

• Pattern matching requires extra fabric।

• Repeat size noted on pattern।

• Notches help align during sewing।

• Marker efficiency drops 10-15%।

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

✅ अध्याय सारांश
Pattern Making: Block → Style Pattern → Grade → Marker। Basic Block exact body measurements पर। Grade Rules हर size change का define करते हैं। Marker efficiency = fabric cost savings। Computer grading और marker much faster और more efficient।

अध्याय 8

Cutting: Fabric Inspection से Bundling तक

Cutting: Fabric Inspection से Bundling तक

Cutting — एक गलती हज़ारों pieces में multiply होती है

📌 अध्याय का उद्देश्य
Fabric inspection system, spreading techniques, cutting methods और wastage control।

8.1 — Cutting Section: Factory का पहला Manufacturing Department

Cutting Section वह जगह है जहाँ fabric → pieces में convert होता है। यह production का सबसे critical first step है।

क्यों critical है:

Cutting में जो गलत हुआ — वह downstream में multiply होता है। Wrong measurement → सभी cut pieces wrong। Wrong shade grouping → shade variation in finished garment।

Cutting Section की responsibilities:

Fabric receipt और inspection। Fabric relaxation (shrinkage के लिए)। Shade sorting। Spreading। Marker placement। Cutting। Sorting and bundling। Numbering। Issue to sewing।

Cutting Department में क्या होना चाहिए:

Cutting tables (long, flat, smooth)। Spreading machines या manual spreading। Cutting machines। Bundling area। Storage for cut components।

Cutting की speed और accuracy का balance:

Too fast = errors, miscuts। Too slow = production bottleneck। Skilled cutter = right balance।

Cutting floor की organization:

Systematic flow: Fabric store → inspection → relaxation → spreading → cutting → bundling → issue।

Unorganized cutting = major production headaches।

8.2 — Fabric Inspection: 4-Point System

Fabric कोई भी हो — अच्छे supplier का भी — inspect ज़रूर करें।

क्यों inspect:

Fabric defects जो cutting के बाद पता चलें = material waste। Defects जो garment में दिखें = rejection, rework। Prevention at source = savings।

4-Point Inspection System (ASTM D5430):

यह industry standard system है।

Fabric roll को inspection frame पर feed करते हैं। Light from behind भी होती है। Inspector defects देखता है और record करता है।

Defects को size के अनुसार points मिलते हैं:

Defect Size | Points Assigned

Up to 3 inches | 1 point

3 inches to 6 inches | 2 points

6 inches to 9 inches | 3 points

Over 9 inches | 4 points

Note: Any defect over 9 inches = 4 points maximum।

Acceptance Level:

Generally: ≤ 40 points per 100 linear yards।

Formula: Total Points × 3600 ÷ (Yards inspected × Fabric width in inches)

अगर calculation ≤ 40: Accept। > 40: Reject या negotiate।

Common Fabric Defects:

Weaving Defects: Missing ends (ताना धागा गायब), Missing picks (बाना धागा गायब), Floats (extra thread), Holes।

Dyeing/Printing Defects: Shade variation, Uneven dyeing, Stripes, Patches।

Finishing Defects: Stains, Oil marks, Crease marks, Press marks।

Fabric Test (before cutting):

Shrinkage Test: 50 cm × 50 cm sample। Wash (as per garment care instructions)। Dry। Re-measure। % shrinkage = (original - final) / original × 100।

If shrinkage > 3% (woven) or > 5% (knit) → Pre-shrink fabric before cutting। या जब customer को inform करें।

Color Fastness Test:

Rub Test (Dry): White cloth से rub करें — color transfer? Acceptable? Wet rub test भी करें।

Wash Test: Fabric wash करके shade change? Adjacent fabric stain?

8.3 — Fabric Relaxation और Shade Sorting

Fabric Relaxation:

Fabric roll को cutting से पहले unfurl करके spreading table पर 24-48 hours छोड़ना। Fabric relax होती है, tension release होती है।

क्यों ज़रूरी:

Fabric in roll में tension होती है। बिना relaxation के cut करें → garment में shrinkage और distortion।

Especially important for: Knit fabrics (high tension in roll), Tightly woven fabrics, Fabrics prone to shrinkage।

Relaxation Area: Dedicated space। Clean floor या tables। Sufficient time।

Shade Sorting:

एक fabric purchase order में आए different rolls में shade slightly different हो सकता है। यह normal है — पर इसे manage करना ज़रूरी है।

Shade बैंड बनाएँ: Roll numbers को shade groups में sort करें।

Example: Shade Band A, B, C।

Rule: एक garment के सभी pieces same shade band से cut करें।

Numbering से enforce करें (section 8.5 में)।

Shade variation के consequences:

एक garment में अगर body एक shade से और sleeve दूसरे shade से cut हो → garment reject।

Customer complaint।

Prevention: Strict shade grouping + numbering system।

8.4 — Spreading: Layers बिछाना

Spreading = Fabric को cutting table पर multiple layers में बिछाना।

Spreading Methods:

Face-to-Face (F/F) Spreading:

Alternate layers fold करते हुए। Symmetrical pieces के लिए excellent। Face to face = mirror images।

Use: When garment has symmetrical panels।

Limitation: Cannot use for pieces with nap direction या one-way pattern।

Face-One-Way (FOW) Spreading:

सभी layers same direction। Face up या face down।

Use: Napped fabric (velvet), one-way designs, asymmetrical pieces।

More wastage than F/F for some styles।

Open Width Spreading:

Tubular knit fabric को cut करके open करना।

Only option for some knit fabrics।

Spreading Quality:

Tension: Zero tension spread करें। Tight spread → garment पर tension release होगी = distortion।

Edge Alignment: सभी layers की edge aligned।

Ply Count: Required number of layers।

Splice: Joins in middle of lay — minimize करें।

Lay Height Considerations:

Woven fabric: 100-200 layers technically possible पर practically 60-150।

Knit fabric: Lighter → more layers; 50-100 typical।

Heavy fabric (denim): 20-50 layers।

Higher lay = more pieces per cut = more efficient। पर height बढ़ने पर bottom layers distorted हो सकते हैं।

Spreading Equipment:

Manual: 2 workers spreading by hand।

Spreading machine: Mechanized। Consistent tension। Large factories में।

8.5 — Cutting, Bundling और Wastage Control

Cutting Machines:

Straight Knife Machine: Most common। 6"-12" vertical blade। Speed: 2000-3500 RPM। Good for straight cuts and mild curves। Up to 12 cm lay height typically।

Round Knife Machine: Circular blade. Good for straight cuts. Lightweight fabric. Small quantities।

Band Knife Machine: Fixed table knife, fabric moved to blade। Excellent for curves। Used for re-cutting individual pieces। Must-have for export factories।

Die Cutting: Metal die, hydraulic press। Very fast for small identical pieces (collar tips, cuffs)। High setup cost।

Cutting Quality:

Blade sharpness: Dull blade = rough edges, displacement. Change/sharpen regularly।

Knife tilt: Blade must be perfectly vertical। Tilt = top layers cut differently from bottom।

Clamps: Secure lay at ends to prevent slippage।

Speed: Consistent speed on curves।

Bundling:

Bundle = एक garment के सभी cut pieces का set।

Bundle size: Generally 10-20 pieces per bundle।

Bundle ticket: Size, style, color, date, bundle number, operator name (blank)।

Components per bundle: All pieces for that garment — front, back, collar, sleeves, cuffs, pocket।

Numbering:

हर piece पर shade identification number stamp करें।

Top layer से bottom layer: 1, 2, 3...

Same number within one garment।

Wastage Calculation और Control:

End Bit Waste: Marker end के बाद बचा fabric।

Selvedge Waste: Fabric edges।

Splice Waste: Lay joins।

Defect Waste: Defective fabric removed।

Total Wastage % = (Total fabric received - Fabric in cut pieces) ÷ Total fabric received × 100

Target: 5-8% for woven, 8-12% for knit।

Wastage कम करने के strategies:

Efficient marker: 88%+ efficiency।

Proper spreading: No excess margins।

Small piece nesting: Collar, cuffs, pockets fill gaps।

Fabric defect mapping: Defects को marker में place करके exclude करें।

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

विस्तृत अध्ययन: Cutting — Advanced Operations

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Cutting section में efficiency और accuracy दोनों critical हैं। A skilled cutting team can significantly reduce fabric waste and defects.

Spreading Table Requirements:

Surface: Smooth, non-slip। Wood or laminate top।

Length: Minimum 6 meters (longer for large orders)।

Width: Slightly wider than maximum fabric width (typically 60-65")।

Height: 85-90 cm (ergonomic standing height)।

Edge markers: At regular intervals (every 50 cm or 1 m)।

Pin/clip holders: Along edges।

Spreader (Manual):

2 workers recommended for smooth spreading।

Roll mounted on spreader at one end।

Fabric pulled gently, evenly।

Edges aligned at one side (and optionally both)।

Semi-automatic spreader:

Motorized roll feed।

Consistent tension।

Faster than manual।

Investment: ₹50,000-2,00,000।

Fabric Lay — Planning and Documentation:

Lay Plan = Document specifying:

Style and size mix।

Number of pieces per size।

Fabric width।

Number of layers (plies)।

Lay direction (F/F or FOW)।

Splice positions (where joins occur)।

Marker number to be used।

Lay Plan must be checked by supervisor before spreading begins।

Cutting Accuracy Standards:

Deviation from cut line: ±2mm acceptable।

Notch depth: 5-7mm typically, ±1mm।

Drill hole: ±2mm from specified position।

To achieve this:

Blade must be sharp (daily for straight knife, as needed for round)।

Knife vertical (not tilted)।

Adequate lay clamps।

Consistent speed (especially on curves)।

Bundling System — Complete Process:

After cutting:

1. Sort pieces by size।

2. Check quantity per size — count actual vs plan।

3. Bundle assembly: All pieces for one garment together।

4. Count pieces per bundle: 10-20 standard।

5. Bundle ticket preparation।

6. Bundle stacking।

7. Issue to sewing with bundle register।

Bundle Ticket Content:

Order number।

Style name।

Size।

Bundle number।

Pieces in bundle।

Color/shade code।

Date cut।

Cutting pair: Some operations need to be in pairs (left sleeve + right sleeve)।

Common Cutting Defects and Prevention:

Defect 1: Blade tilt (pieces narrower at bottom than top)

Cause: Knife leaning।

Prevention: Check knife verticality regularly। Keep blade sharp।

Defect 2: Notch too deep

Cause: Over-cutting।

Prevention: Controlled notch depth。 Use notching guide।

Defect 3: Misalignment between plies

Cause: Improper spreading, slippage।

Prevention: Adequate clamps। Check alignment during spreading।

Defect 4: Pieces cut off-grain

Cause: Marker not straight, spreading not straight।

Prevention: Straight spreading, marker aligned to grain。

Defect 5: Shade mixing

Cause: Bundles mixed before numbering।

Prevention: Number immediately after cutting, before moving।

Cutting Productivity:

Measuring cutting productivity:

Pieces cut per day per cutter (depends on lay height and style complexity)।

Better metric: Metres spread and cut per day।

Industry benchmarks (indicative):

Manual spreading: 300-500 m/day (2 workers)।

Semi-auto spreading: 600-1000 m/day।

Cutting (straight knife, 100 layers): 500-800 m/day।

CAM cutting: 1000-2000 m/day।

Shade Variation Management — Complete System:

Step 1: Receive fabric — shade number on each roll from supplier।

Step 2: Inspect — confirm shade consistency within batch।

Step 3: Separate shade batches if mixed delivery।

Step 4: Assign shade letters: A, B, C।

Step 5: Lay planning — all layers from same shade group।

Step 6: Numbering: Sequential within each shade group।

Step 7: Sewing: Bundle tickets must match (don't mix shade A bundle with shade B bundle)।

Step 8: Finishing: Shade check before packing (front and back from same shade)।

Shade variation that passes QC:

Panels within one garment: Zero shade variation acceptable।

Garment to garment in one order: Very slight variation may be acceptable (buyer-dependent)।

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

✅ अध्याय सारांश
Cutting Section: Inspection → Relaxation → Shade Sort → Spread → Cut → Bundle → Number → Issue। 4-Point Inspection system defects catch करता है। Shade grouping + Numbering shade variation prevent करता है। Marker efficiency = fabric wastage = cost।

अध्याय 9

Sewing Operations: Garment का Complete Operation Breakdown

Sewing Operations: Garment का Complete Operation Breakdown

एक Shirt कितने operations में बनती है — Industrial sewing की depth

📌 अध्याय का उद्देश्य
Complete operation breakdown, machine assignment, sewing sequence का importance और quality points per operation।

9.1 — Operation Breakdown: Manufacturing का DNA

Operation Breakdown = एक complete garment को individual, distinct, measurable operations में divide करना।

यह document Garment Manufacturing का सबसे fundamental technical document है।

Operation Breakdown से क्या मिलता है:

1. हर operation का Individual time (SMV)।

2. हर operation के लिए सही machine।

3. Line balancing का आधार।

4. Training plan का आधार।

5. Quality check points।

6. Operation Bulletin (production bible)।

Operation Breakdown के बिना:

कौन कितना time लेता है? पता नहीं।

Line कहाँ slow है? पता नहीं।

Production target कितना होना चाहिए? Guess।

Efficiency कैसे calculate करें? Cannot।

Operation Breakdown के साथ:

सब measurable है। Management data-driven है। Improvement possible है।

Important: हर style का अलग Operation Breakdown। Style बदले → Operations बदल सकते हैं।

9.2 — Men's Formal Shirt का Complete Operation Breakdown

यह industry में सबसे standard garment है — इसका breakdown सीखें, बाकी समझ में आएंगे।

Men's Formal Shirt — Complete Operation List:

PREPARATION SECTION (Pre-sewing):

P01. Fabric spreading और cutting

P02. Front Placket — Fusing

P03. Collar Inner — Fusing

P04. Collar Stand — Fusing

P05. Cuff — Fusing

P06. Bundle check और issue

SEWING SECTION — FRONT BODY:

S01. Front Placket — Iron/fold

S02. Front Placket — Attach (Left side)

S03. Front Placket — Topstitch (Left side)

S04. Front Placket — Make and Attach (Right side — box placket)

S05. Front Pocket — Mark position

S06. Front Pocket — Sew and turn

S07. Front Pocket — Attach (topstitch 3 sides)

S08. Front Body — Button hole marking (if before attach)

SEWING SECTION — BACK BODY:

S09. Back Yoke — Attach (first seam)

S10. Back Yoke — Fold and close (second seam — pin tuck or plain)

S11. Back Pleat — Make (center back)

SEWING SECTION — BODY JOIN:

S12. Shoulder Seam — Join Front + Back

S13. Shoulder Seam — Overlock (if not safety stitch)

SEWING SECTION — COLLAR:

S14. Collar — Under Collar and Upper Collar join (three sides)

S15. Collar — Trim corners और Turn

S16. Collar — Press/Shape

S17. Collar — Topstitch (edge stitching)

S18. Collar Stand — Join to Collar (inner seam)

S19. Collar Stand — Topstitch और trim

SEWING SECTION — SLEEVE:

S20. Sleeve Placket — Make (continuous lap)

S21. Sleeve Placket — Attach

S22. Sleeve Bottom Hem — Prepare (gather if needed)

SEWING SECTION — JOIN SLEEVE:

S23. Sleeve — Attach to Body (set-in method)

S24. Sleeve Seam + Side Seam — Join (continuous or separate)

S25. Side Seam — Overlock

SEWING SECTION — COLLAR ATTACH:

S26. Collar Stand — Attach to Neckline (inside)

S27. Collar Stand — Fold and topstitch (outside)

S28. Collar Stand — End stitching

SEWING SECTION — CUFF:

S29. Cuff — Make (join outer and inner)

S30. Cuff — Turn और Press

S31. Cuff — Topstitch

S32. Cuff — Attach to sleeve

FINISHING OPERATIONS:

S33. Bottom Hem — Fold and sew

S34. Label — Attach (brand, size, care labels)

BUTTONHOLE & BUTTONS:

B01. Buttonhole — Front placket (5-6 holes)

B02. Buttonhole — Cuffs (1 each)

B03. Buttonhole — Collar stand (1)

B04. Button Attach — Front placket

B05. Button Attach — Cuffs

B06. Button Attach — Collar stand (spare button)

FINISHING SECTION:

F01. Thread Trimming — Full garment

F02. Press — Full garment

F03. Final Inspection

F04. Size Tag — Attach

F05. Hang Tag — Attach

F06. Fold + Insert collar support

F07. Poly Bag

F08. Pack in carton

Total Operations: ~40-45 (style variations के according)।

9.3 — Machine Assignment और Sequence का Logic

Operation Bulletin में हर operation के लिए machine specify होती है।

Machine Assignment Table (Partial — Men's Shirt):

Operation | Machine Type

Front Placket Attach | SNLS (1/16" piping foot)

Pocket Attach | SNLS (standard foot)

Shoulder Seam | Safety Stitch (4-thread overlock)

Collar Make | SNLS

Collar Topstitch | SNLS (Hinged foot)

Sleeve Attach | SNLS

Side Seam | Safety Stitch

Collar Attach | SNLS

Cuff Attach | SNLS

Buttonhole | Buttonhole Machine

Button Attach | Button Attach Machine

Thread Trimming | Thread Sucker Machine या manual

Sequence का Critical Importance:

गलत sequence = rework। उदाहरण:

गलत sequence: Side seam sew करें → फिर label attach करने की कोशिश।

Problem: Label access बहुत difficult। Quality poor।

Correct: Label attach, फिर side seam।

गलत sequence: Collar attach कर दें → फिर collar topstitch।

Problem: At this point, topstitch difficult।

Correct: Collar topstitch पहले, फिर body पर attach।

Golden Rule: Inside operations पहले। जो operation बाद में access करना मुश्किल होगा — वह पहले।

Quality Points per Operation:

हर operation पर specific quality check:

Operation: Pocket Attach

• Position: 2 cm from armhole (as spec)

• Centered: Horizontal

• Stitch: 3.5 SPC, straight

• Start/End: Reverse stitch

• Corners: Square, no puckering

• Thread: Matching color

Operation: Collar Stand Attach

• Center back aligned

• Stand equal on both sides

• Stitch in ditch (not visible from outside)

• No puckering at corners

• Collar spreads evenly

Inline QC using Operation Checklist:

10th piece, 20th piece, 30th piece — operator self-check।

Supervisor hourly random check।

QC checker end-line।

9.4 — Rework को Minimize करना: Zero Defect Culture

Rework = किया हुआ काम दोबारा करना। यह time, cost और motivation तीनों खाता है।

Rework Cost Calculation:

एक operation का SMV: 1.5 min

Rework करने का समय: 3 min (open + redo)

Extra cost: 2× labour cost for that operation

Plus: Missed production target

Plus: QC checker time

5 रुपये की कोशिश = 15-20 रुपये का rework।

Defects के प्रमुख कारण:

Operator Errors:

Wrong stitch length।

Wrong tension।

Speed too fast for curves।

Not following SOP।

Machine Problems:

Tension incorrect।

Needle blunt।

Feed dog problem।

Broken thread।

Material Problems:

Fabric slippery।

Mismarked components।

Wrong thread color।

Management Problems:

No proper training।

No SOP।

No inline check।

Pressure for speed over quality।

Defect Prevention System:

Pre-production:

• SOP documented और available।

• Operators trained।

• Machines serviced।

• Thread and needle checked।

Production:

• First piece check by supervisor।

• Every 10th piece operator self-check।

• Hourly supervisor random check।

• 10-piece bundle check by inline QC।

Reaction:

• Problem identified → STOP.

• Root cause find करें।

• Fix करें।

• फिर start करें।

DHU Targets:

World Class Factory: < 3 DHU।

Good Factory: 3-8 DHU।

Average: 8-15 DHU।

Poor: > 15 DHU।

अपना DHU track करें और weekly trend देखें।

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

विस्तृत अध्ययन: Sewing Operations — Expert Level

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Sewing operations को expert level पर समझने के लिए न केवल "क्या सिला जाता है" बल्कि "कैसे, किस machine पर, किस quality standard के साथ" — यह सब जानना ज़रूरी है।

Operation Bulletin — Complete Format:

Factory: _______ | Style: _______ | Season: _______ | Date: _______

Buyer: _______ | Target: _____ pieces/day | Total SMV: _____ min

OP # | OPERATION | MACHINE | ATTACHMENT | NEEDLE | THREAD | SPC | SMV | QUALITY POINTS | ILLUSTRATION

Example entries:

01 | Front Placket — Fold & Edge | SNLS | Guide | 80 | 40/2 | 3.5 | 0.45 | Width 3.5 cm ±0.2 | [Sketch]

02 | Front Placket — Attach | SNLS | Piping foot | 80 | 40/2 | 3.5 | 0.80 | Straight, both ends reverse, no puckering | [Sketch]

03 | Pocket — Make | SNLS + Iron | Box guide | 80 | 40/2 | 3.5 | 0.55 | Square corners, no puckering | [Sketch]

Total operations: 28 | Total SMV: 18.5 min

Approved by: Production Manager _______ | IE Manager _______ | Date: _______

Operation Illustrations:

Each operation should have a small sketch showing:

• Start and end points।

• Direction of sewing।

• Any critical measurements।

• Quality check points highlighted।

These help visual learners and reduce training time।

Thread Consumption Calculation:

Thread length per garment is important for costing and purchasing।

Empirical rule: Thread length = 3-4× seam length for lockstitch, 8-10× for overlock।

For a standard shirt:

Seam analysis:

- Collar seams: ~2m total seam length

- Collar topstitch: ~1.5m

- Shoulder seams: ~0.6m each, total 1.2m

- Sleeve seams: ~1m each, total 2m

- Side seams: ~1.2m each, total 2.4m

- Bottom hem: ~1.4m

- Other operations: ~3m

Total seam length: ~13.7m

Thread consumed (lockstitch): 13.7 × 3.5 = 48m (upper + lower combined: 96m)

Thread consumed (overlock, multiply by 10): 3m × 10 = 30m

Total thread: ~126m per garment।

Standard cone: 5,000m - 10,000m।

One cone for ~40-80 shirts।

For 100 shirts: 3 cones approx।

Seam Types — Engineering Perspective:

Seam is the basic structural unit of a garment।

PLAIN SEAM (1.01.01):

Two pieces joined, seam allowance inside।

Most common।

Strength: Depends on stitch and seam allowance।

Used: Most seams in woven garments।

FRENCH SEAM (1.06.01):

Two stitching lines, raw edge enclosed।

Strong and neat।

Time: 2× plain seam time।

Used: Lightweight, sheer fabrics। Children's garments।

FLAT FELL SEAM (2.01.01):

One seam folded over other, topstitched।

Very strong, flat appearance।

Seen on: Jeans outseam। Men's shirt yoke।

Time: 3× plain seam।

BOUND SEAM (1.06.03):

Seam allowance bound with separate strip।

Neat, strong。

Used: Unlined jackets, reversible garments।

Time: 4× plain seam।

SERGED/OVERLOCKED SEAM (5.01):

Seam + edge finish in one pass।

Fast, efficient।

Used: T-shirts, casual garments.

For each product, specify which seam type at which location।

Sewing Quality Standards — Measurable Criteria:

Stitch count per cm/inch:

Light fabric: 4-5 SPC (stitches per cm)।

Medium: 3.5-4 SPC।

Heavy: 3-3.5 SPC।

Seam strength: Pull test। Minimum per buyer spec।

Example: 18N (Newtons) for main seams। Children's: 25N।

Seam width (seam allowance):

Shoulder, armhole: 1 cm ± 2mm।

Side seams: 1.5 cm ± 2mm।

Start/end fastening: 3-4 reverse stitches, stitches within seam।

Thread tails: Maximum 1 cm visible (or trimmed flush)।

Straight stitch tolerance: ±2mm deviation per 10 cm।

These measurable standards make quality objective — no subjective arguments।

Operator Training — Skill Building Techniques:

For a new operator on a specific operation:

Week 1: Practice without machine running (understand motion)।

Week 2: Slow speed — accuracy over speed।

Week 3: Progressive speed increase।

Week 4: Normal speed, quality check।

Week 5-8: Consistency building।

Week 9-12: Full efficiency target।

Daily training drill:

15 pieces in morning: Pure practice (not counted in production)।

Then: Full production with quality targets।

Visual training aids:

Photo of good stitch and bad stitch — posted at workstation।

Measurement guide at workstation।

Common defects sheet — how to identify and prevent।

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

✅ अध्याय सारांश
Operation Breakdown Men's Shirt में 40-45 operations। हर operation के लिए specific machine, sequence, quality points। Sequence wrong = rework। Inline QC से defects source पर catch करें। DHU track करें, target < 5।

PART III — Limited Sewing
अध्याय 10

Limited Sewing: Framework और Implementation

Limited Sewing: Framework और Implementation

Focus की शक्ति — कम variety से ज़्यादा mastery

📌 अध्याय का उद्देश्य
Limited Sewing model को define करना, implement करना और profitably चलाना।

10.1 — Limited Sewing: एक Original Framework

"Limited Sewing" एक concept है जो इस पुस्तक में specifically define किया गया है — यह Tailoring और Mass Production के बीच का एक practical, achievable bridge है।

Definition:

Limited Sewing एक production model है जिसमें:

• सीमित number of designs (3-5 core products)

• Standard measurements (defined size chart)

• सीमित fabric varieties (3-5 fabric types/colors)

• Standard operations (SOP documented)

• Repeat orders पर focus

• Small but consistent batch sizes

• Controlled inventory

यह neither purely Tailoring है (individual custom orders) nor Mass Production (thousands per day)।

यह क्यों important है:

Tailoring में growth ceiling बहुत low है। एक व्यक्ति एक दिन में limited pieces बना सकता है।

Mass Production में entry barrier बहुत high है। Capital, systems, workers — सब बड़े scale पर चाहिए।

Limited Sewing उस gap को fill करता है।

Limited Sewing किसके लिए ideal है:

घर से काम करने वाली महिला उद्यमी। एक दर्जी जो systematically grow करना चाहता है। Small unit जो chaos reduce करना चाहता है। Boutique जो repeat business चाहती है। SHG/Cooperative जो institutional supply करना चाहता है।

Economics of Limited Sewing:

एक महिला जो unlimited varieties बनाती है: Average 3-4 pieces/day (constant setup, new patterns)।

वही महिला जो 2 standard products बनाती है: Average 8-12 pieces/day (practice, speed, no setup)।

Income: 2-3× higher with same effort।

यही Limited Sewing की power है।

10.2 — Limited Sewing के 6 Core Elements

Element 1: Limited Designs

3-5 core products maximum। हर season में 1-2 नए explore कर सकते हैं, पर core stable रखें।

"Hero Products" identify करें — वे products जो सबसे ज़्यादा और सबसे consistently बिकते हैं। उनपर maximum focus।

Hero product criteria:

• Consistent year-round demand।

• Standard sizes available।

• Material easily sourced।

• Operations repeatable।

• Profit margin acceptable।

School uniforms, basic kurtas, ladies salwar, men's shirts — ये classic hero products हैं।

Element 2: Standard Measurements

Size chart develop करें और freeze करें। S, M, L, XL minimum। Product के अनुसार और sizes।

Pattern एक बार बनाएँ — reuse करें। Pattern बदलने की ज़रूरत तभी हो जब:

• Buyer new spec दे।

• Fit feedback आए।

• Style change हो।

Element 3: Limited Fabric Varieties

5-7 fabric types maximum। प्रत्येक fabric के 2-3 core colors।

Benefits:

• Bulk buying → better rates।

• Staff familiar with handling।

• Storage simple।

• Waste management easy।

Avoid: "Customer demand पर कोई भी fabric लाएंगे" — यह chaos है।

Element 4: Standard Operations

हर product के लिए Operation Bulletin। SOP document।

Operator को एक product की operations master करने दें — speed और quality दोनों improve होंगी।

Element 5: Repeat Orders

Single orders less efficient। Repeat orders = efficiency, familiarity, speed।

Repeat Order Sources:

• Institutions (schools, hospitals, factories)

• Regular retailers

• Online channels (your own or marketplace)

• Government supply contracts

Element 6: Controlled Inventory

Raw material: Confirmed order के बाद ही buy। Safety stock minimal।

WIP: 1-2 days production maximum।

Finished goods: Ship within 48 hours of completion।

No dead stock rule: अगर कोई fabric/material 3 months से use नहीं हुई — evaluate और dispose करें।

10.3 — Limited Sewing Setup: Step by Step

Step 1: Self Assessment (Week 1)

Current situation honestly evaluate करें:

• Machines available (type, condition)

• Skills available (your own + workers)

• Space available

• Working capital available

• Time available (part-time या full-time)

• Network (who will buy?)

Step 2: Market Research (Week 1-2)

Local market में जाएँ:

• Schools: Uniform requirements? Current supplier? Satisfied?

• Hospitals: Staff uniform requirement?

• Corporate offices: Work wear?

• Retail shops: What moves fast?

• Online: What's selling on local channels?

Talk to minimum 10 potential buyers।

Step 3: Product Selection (Week 2)

Based on:

• Your skills

• Local demand

• Competition

• Profit potential

Select 1-2 products to start। Not more।

Step 4: Sample Development (Week 2-3)

Sample बनाएँ। Quality excellent होना चाहिए।

Present to 2-3 potential buyers।

Get feedback।

Revise if needed।

Step 5: First Order (Week 3-4)

Minimum order accept करें।

Deliver excellent।

Get testimonial/review।

Ask for referral।

Step 6: Systematize (Month 2-3)

Pattern finalize करें।

Operations document करें।

Basic costing करें।

Pricing decide करें।

Order register बनाएँ।

Step 7: Scale (Month 3+)

Second worker hire करें।

Second machine add करें।

More institutional customers।

10.4 — Product Selection Matrix

सही product select करना Limited Sewing की सबसे important decision है।

Product Evaluation Framework:

Score each factor 1-5 (1=poor, 5=excellent):

DEMAND (20 points):

Year-round demand? (1-5)

Local demand proven? (1-5)

Competition not too intense? (1-5)

Growing market? (1-5)

OPERATIONS (20 points):

Operations standard? (1-5)

Can be done with current machines? (1-5)

Learning curve reasonable? (1-5)

Quality controllable? (1-5)

ECONOMICS (20 points):

Material easily sourced? (1-5)

Profit margin >15%? (1-5)

Payment terms acceptable? (1-5)

Volume potential? (1-5)

REPEAT (15 points):

Repeat purchase likely? (1-5)

Institutional buyer potential? (1-5)

Season-independent? (1-5)

COMPETITION (5 points):

Differentiation possible? (1-5)

Total Score out of 80:

64+: Excellent choice।

50-63: Good choice।

36-49: Consider carefully।

Below 36: Avoid।

Top Products for Limited Sewing in India:

School Uniforms: Consistent, institutional, year-round.

Hospital Uniforms: High volume, standard specs.

Corporate Work Wear: Bulk orders, seasonal.

Ladies Churidar-Kameez: Wide demand, multiple sizes.

Men's Kurta-Pajama: Ethnic wear, stable demand.

Children's Wear (School): Age-based sizing, repeat purchase.

10.5 — Costing और Pricing in Limited Sewing

Limited Sewing में costing accurate होनी चाहिए — यही business की sustainability है।

Complete Costing for School Shirt (Size M) — Indicative Example:

MATERIAL COST:

Fabric consumption: 1.5 m per piece

Fabric rate: ₹85/m

Fabric cost: ₹127.50

Wastage (8%): ₹10.20

Fabric total: ₹137.70

Buttons (5 nos @ ₹1.5): ₹7.50

Thread (estimated): ₹3.00

Interlining (collar, cuff): ₹7.00

Labels (size + care): ₹3.00

Trims total: ₹20.50

TOTAL MATERIAL COST: ₹158.20

LABOUR COST:

Workers: 3 (1 owner working, 2 paid)

Paid workers wage: 2 × ₹350/day = ₹700/day

Owner's time value: ₹350/day equivalent

Total daily labour: ₹1,050

Daily production: 35 pieces

Labour per piece: ₹1,050 ÷ 35 = ₹30.00

OVERHEAD (per piece):

Rent: ₹3,000/month ÷ 700 pieces = ₹4.30

Electricity: ₹800/month ÷ 700 = ₹1.14

Machine depreciation: ₹1,500/month ÷ 700 = ₹2.14

Maintenance: ₹500/month ÷ 700 = ₹0.71

Miscellaneous: ₹700/month ÷ 700 = ₹1.00

Total Overhead per piece: ₹9.29

PACKAGING:

Poly bag + tag: ₹4.00

TOTAL COST: ₹158.20 + ₹30.00 + ₹9.29 + ₹4.00 = ₹201.49

DESIRED PROFIT (15%): ₹30.22

SELLING PRICE (to institution): ₹231.71 → Round to ₹235

At ₹235:

Revenue (700 pieces × ₹235): ₹1,64,500

Total cost: ₹1,41,043

Monthly profit: ₹23,457

⚠️ ये indicative numbers हैं। Actual costs स्थान, scale और current market rates पर depend करते हैं।

Pricing Strategy:

Institutional buyers: Direct price। Negotiate above your minimum।

Retail: Add 20-25% over institutional।

Online: Add 30-40% — customer acquisition cost cover करें।

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

✅ अध्याय सारांश
Limited Sewing = Focus + Standard + Repeat। 6 core elements। Product selection matrix से सही product choose करें। Accurate costing = sustainable business। Institutional buyers = most reliable repeat customers।

PART IV — Small Batch Production
अध्याय 11

Batch Production: Individual Order से Batch Thinking तक

Batch Production: Individual Order से Batch Thinking तक

Batch Production — efficiency की पहली असली सीढ़ी

11.1 — Batch Production क्या है और क्यों ज़रूरी है

Batch Production = एक design की एक निश्चित quantity को एक organised, systematic process में एक साथ बनाना।

Individual order: Mrs. Sharma की blue salwar → अलग से बनाओ। Mrs. Verma की green salwar → फिर नई शुरुआत।

Batch Production: 100 blue salwars (S:20, M:40, L:30, XL:10) → एक साथ, एक process में।

Batch Production क्यों better है:

Setup Time Saving: Pattern एक बार, cutting एक बार। 100 pieces के लिए setup time virtually same as 1 piece। प्रति piece setup cost = 1/100।

Material Efficiency: एक fabric lot, एक marker। Wastage कम।

Operator Speed (Learning Curve): जब operator same operation 100 बार करता है — speed बढ़ती है, quality consistent होती है। Individual orders में यह नहीं मिलता।

Quality Consistency: Same operations, same machine, same thread, same time → consistent output।

Costing Accuracy: Batch के बाद exact cost per piece निकाल सकते हैं।

Inventory Predictability: Batch complete = delivery possible। कोई "almost ready" की situation नहीं।

Learning Curve Theory:

जब production बढ़ती है — per unit time घटता है।

80% learning curve: Production double होने पर time 20% कम होता है।

100 pieces: 25 min/piece average

200 pieces (cumulative): 20 min/piece

400 pieces: 16 min/piece

Batch production इस learning curve को exploit करता है।

11.2 — Batch Size का Scientific चुनाव

Batch size determine करना एक important business decision है।

Factors जो Batch Size को affect करते हैं:

1. Order Quantity: Customer ने कितने चाहिए?

2. Production Capacity: रोज़ कितने बन सकते हैं?

3. Delivery Date: कब चाहिए?

4. Working Capital: एक साथ कितना material खरीद सकते हैं?

5. Storage Space: Finished goods कहाँ रखेंगे?

6. Cash Flow: Payment कब आएगा?

Batch Size Calculation:

Formula: Batch Size = min(Order Qty, Capacity × Lead Time)

Example 1 — School Uniform Order:

Order: 500 shirts

Capacity: 40 shirts/day

Delivery: 15 working days

Maximum possible: 40 × 15 = 600 shirts

Batch Size Decision: All 500 in one batch (fits within timeline)

Example 2 — Large Export Order:

Order: 5000 shirts

Capacity: 200 shirts/day

Delivery: 30 working days

Maximum possible: 200 × 30 = 6000 (fits)

Working Capital Constraint: Can buy material for 1000 pieces only

Decision: 5 batches of 1000 pieces (rolling production)

Optimal Batch Size for Small Units:

Too Small (10-20 pieces): High setup cost per piece। Frequent changeovers।

Too Large (1000+ pieces): Working capital blocked। Storage issue। Long cycle time।

Optimal for small unit: 50-200 pieces per batch।

EOQ (Economic Order Quantity) concept:

Balances: Setup cost (decreases with larger batch) + Holding cost (increases with larger batch)

EOQ = √(2 × Annual Demand × Setup Cost / Holding Cost per unit per year)

Small units के लिए: Practical judgement + the formula as guidance।

11.3 — Batch Planning और Production Flow

Batch Planning Sheet एक simple document है जो entire batch को manage करता है।

Batch Planning Sheet Format:

Batch Number: B-2025-045

Style: School Shirt - White

Size Break: S-30, M-100, L-80, XL-40 | Total: 250

Customer: ABC School, Bhopal

Order Date: 01-Nov-2025

Start Date: 03-Nov-2025

Target Completion: 15-Nov-2025

Actual Completion: ___________

Material Planning:

Fabric (main): 250 × 1.6m = 400m + wastage 8% = 432m

Fabric (interlining): 250 × 0.15m = 37.5m → order 40m

Buttons: 250 × 6 = 1500 nos

Thread: 250 × 80m = 20,000m → approx 20 cones

Labels: 250 × 2 = 500 nos

Production Schedule:

Day 1-2: Fabric inspection + relaxation

Day 3: Cutting

Day 4-11: Sewing (31 shirts/day target)

Day 12-13: Finishing + QC

Day 14: Packing

Day 15: Dispatch

Daily Production Tracking:

Date | Target | Cutting | Sewing | Finishing | QC Pass | Remarks

03/11 | - | 250✓ | - | - | - | -

04/11 | 31 | - | 35 | - | - | Ahead

05/11 | 31 | - | 30 | - | - | On track

...

WIP Tracking:

In Cutting: ___

In Sewing: ___

In Finishing: ___

In QC: ___

Packed: ___

Total Tracked: ___ (should = 250)

यह simple tracking system WIP को visible बनाता है।

11.4 — Batch Production में Common Problems

Problem 1: Mid-Batch Material Shortage

Situation: 150 pieces बन गए, बाकी 100 के लिए fabric short।

Causes: BOM में calculation error। Wastage expected से ज़्यादा। Fabric defects।

Solution: 5% buffer fabric always order करें। Incoming inspection में shrinkage test। Accurate BOM।

Problem 2: Quality Inconsistency across Batch

Situation: पहले 50 pieces perfect, अगले 50 में defects।

Causes: Machine problem mid-batch। Worker fatigue। Thread change। Fabric shade variation।

Solution: Regular machine check during production। Hourly QC। Shade grouping।

Problem 3: Batch Completed but Wrong Size Mix

Situation: S:50, M:50, L:50, XL:50 order था। Cut हुआ S:60, M:40, L:50, XL:50।

Causes: Cutting error। Bundle mix-up।

Solution: Size-wise cutting plan। Separate stacks। Count verify after cutting।

Problem 4: Batch Delay

Situation: 15-day delivery → 20 days।

Causes: Material late। Machine breakdown। Workers absent।

Solution: Buffer days in planning। Material ordered early। Backup suppliers। Machine maintenance।

Problem 5: Customer Rejects Quality

Situation: Batch delivered → customer rejects 30%।

Causes: No proper QC। Inline problems not caught।

Solution: Mandatory inline QC। Final inspection before dispatch। AQL sampling।

Prevention is always cheaper than cure। Plan → Execute → Monitor → Correct।

11.5 — Batch Records और Documentation

Documentation is the backbone of systematic production।

Minimum Documents for Batch Production:

1. Batch Planning Sheet (as above)

2. Cutting Record:

Style | Size | Pieces Cut | Fabric Used | Wastage | Date | Cutter Sign

3. Sewing Production Record:

Date | Operator | Operation | Pieces Completed | Defects | Remarks

4. QC Record:

Batch # | Pieces Inspected | Defects Found | DHU | Pass/Fail | Action | QC Sign

5. Delivery Challan:

To (Customer) | Batch # | Style | Sizes | Quantity | Date | Signature

6. Rework Register:

Batch # | Piece # | Defect Type | Rework By | Date | OK/Reject

Why Documentation matters:

Traceability: अगर customer complaint आए → which batch, which lot of fabric।

Learning: Next batch के लिए क्या improve करें।

Billing: Quantity proof।

Compliance: Government/buyer audit।

Business analysis: Which product most profitable।

Digital vs Paper:

Start: Paper registers, then Excel।

Scale: Cloud-based tools (Google Sheets)।

Large: ERP।

"Jokingly, documentation is boring — until you need it in court."

Seriously: Documentation = professional credibility।

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

✅ अध्याय सारांश
Batch Production: एक design की defined quantity एक साथ। Benefits: Setup savings, learning curve, quality consistency, accurate costing। Optimal batch size: 50-200 pieces for small units। Planning + Daily tracking + Documentation = successful batch।

अध्याय 12

Product Standardisation और Production Planning

Product Standardisation और Production Planning

Standard बनाएँ — Scale करें; Plan करें — Deliver करें

12.1 — Standardisation: Scale का Passport

Standardisation = किसी भी activity, product या process को एक defined, repeatable, measurable format में define करना।

बिना Standardisation के:

हर worker अपने तरीके से काम करता है। हर piece slightly different। Customer expectations unmet। Scale impossible — क्योंकि आप हर नए worker को "your specific way" नहीं सिखा सकते।

Standardisation के साथ:

Defined way of working। Consistent output। New worker को SOP दे दो — वह same quality produce करेगा। Scale possible।

5 Levels of Standardisation in Garment:

Level 1 — Standard Size: Size chart documented। Graded patterns।

Level 2 — Standard Pattern: Master patterns archived। Version controlled।

Level 3 — Standard Process (SOP): हर operation का defined method।

Level 4 — Standard Time (SMV): हर operation का measured time।

Level 5 — Standard Quality: Measurable quality spec — defect definitions, tolerances।

Standardisation Implementation:

Step 1: Document current best practice।

Step 2: Train all workers on standard।

Step 3: Check compliance regularly।

Step 4: Update when improvement found।

Step 5: Never deviate without authorization।

The Cost of Non-Standardisation:

Example: 5 operators sewing pocket, each their own way।

Average time: 1.2 min, 0.9 min, 1.5 min, 1.1 min, 0.8 min।

Mean: 1.1 min, Range: 0.7 min।

Line planning impossible — too variable।

After standardisation (SOP + training):

All operators: 0.95 min ±0.05 min।

Predictable. Plannable. Manageable.

12.2 — Production Planning: पहली बार

Production Planning = Order को Delivery में convert करने का roadmap।

बिना Planning के:

Material अचानक खत्म होती है। Workers idle हैं — कुछ cutting में, कुछ सिलाई में अटके। Delivery miss होती है। Customer angry।

Planning के साथ:

हर दिन का काम पहले से defined। Material timely available। Delivery on time।

Simple Production Planning Process (Small Unit):

Step 1: ORDER ANALYSIS

What: Style, sizes, quantity

When: Delivery date

Any special requirements?

Step 2: CAPACITY CHECK

Daily capacity: Workers × pieces/day/worker

Working days available: Today to delivery

Total capacity = daily × days

क्या order fit होगा?

Step 3: MATERIAL PLANNING

Fabric: Pieces × consumption per piece ÷ (1 - wastage%)

Trims: BOM × quantity

Lead time check: Delivery में कितने दिन?

Order date: Delivery start date - lead time

Step 4: PRODUCTION SCHEDULE

Cutting: Day 1-2

Sewing: Day 3 to N-3

Finishing: N-3 to N-1

QC + Packing: N-2 to N-1

Dispatch: Day N

Step 5: DAILY TARGETS

Total pieces ÷ Available sewing days = Daily target

Build in 10-15% buffer for problems।

Production Planning Example:

Order: 300 Ladies Salwar-Kameez

Delivery: 20 working days

Capacity: 20 pieces/day (3 operators)

Material lead time: 5 days

Buffer: 2 days

Available production days: 20 - 5 (material) - 2 (buffer) = 13 days

Required per day: 300 ÷ 13 = 23 pieces/day

Gap: Need 23/day, have 20/day capacity.

Options:

A: Negotiate 2-3 extra days from customer

B: Add 1 temporary worker

C: Overtime for 3 days

Decision: Option A + partial B।

12.3 — Material Requirement Planning (MRP) — Simplified

MRP = किसी order के लिए exactly कितना material चाहिए, कब चाहिए।

BOM (Bill of Material) — Foundation of MRP:

BOM एक list है जो हर garment के लिए required materials specify करती है।

BOM Example — Ladies Salwar-Kameez Set:

Item | Per Piece | Unit | Notes

Main Fabric (Kameez) | 2.5 | meter | 45" width

Main Fabric (Salwar) | 2.0 | meter | 45" width

Dupatta Fabric | 2.2 | meter | 45" width

Elastic (waist) | 0.8 | meter | 1" width

Lace/Border | 1.5 | meter | Kameez bottom

Thread (main) | 120 | meter | As per stitch count

Thread (border) | 30 | meter | Embroidery/border

Label (size) | 1 | piece |

Label (care) | 1 | piece |

Poly bag | 1 | piece |

Tag | 1 | piece |

Total Fabric per set: 6.7 meters

MRP Calculation for 300 sets:

Fabric (main, all): 300 × 6.7 = 2010 m

Wastage (10%): 201 m

ORDER QUANTITY: 2211 m (order 2250 m rounded)

Elastic: 300 × 0.8 = 240 m

ORDER: 250 m (buffer)

Thread: 300 × 150 = 45,000 m → 50 cones (900m/cone average)

Labels: 300 × 2 = 600 (size + care)

Poly bags: 300 (+ 10 extra = 310)

Procurement Timeline:

Order fabric: 15 days before production start

Thread/small items: 5 days before

Labels: 7 days before (if custom)

MRP Controls:

Never issue more than BOM says — unless authorized।

Shortfall report daily।

Return excess material to store।

12.4 — Batch Costing: Complete Example

Complete Batch Costing — 300 Ladies Salwar-Kameez Sets।

ORDER DETAILS:

Style: Ladies Churidar-Kameez with Dupatta

Quantity: 300 sets

Size Mix: S:60, M:120, L:90, XL:30

Delivery: 20 days

Selling Price (agreed): ₹650/set

MATERIAL COST:

Main Fabric: 2250 m × ₹95/m = ₹2,13,750

Elastic: 250 m × ₹15/m = ₹3,750

Lace/Border: 450 m × ₹25/m = ₹11,250

Thread (all): 50 cones × ₹60 = ₹3,000

Labels: 600 × ₹2 = ₹1,200

Poly Bags: 310 × ₹3 = ₹930

Tags: 300 × ₹2 = ₹600

Subtotal Material: ₹2,34,480

Per set: ₹781.60 → Hmm, this exceeds selling price!

REVIEW:

Fabric cost alone: ₹2,13,750 ÷ 300 = ₹712.50/set

This is more than selling price of ₹650!

• This order is NOT PROFITABLE at this price!

LESSON: Costing BEFORE accepting order। Never agree on price first।

RENEGOTIATION:

Fabric at better bulk rate: ₹88/m (negotiate with supplier)

Fabric cost: 2250 × ₹88 = ₹1,98,000 → per set ₹660

Still over ₹650 without labour and overhead.

Minimum viable selling price calculation:

Material: ₹660

Labour: ₹75

Overhead: ₹40

Packing: ₹10

Total cost: ₹785

Margin 15%: ₹118

Minimum price: ₹903/set

Communicate to buyer: "Our minimum is ₹900/set for this quality."

If buyer says no at ₹900: Politely decline. Better to refuse unprofitable order than lose money।

⚠️ All numbers indicative. Actual costs vary by location, market and time।

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

✅ अध्याय सारांश
Standardisation = Scale का passport। 5 levels: Size, Pattern, Process, Time, Quality। Production Planning: Order analysis → Capacity check → MRP → Schedule। Always cost BEFORE accepting order. Unprofitable order = decline।
📝 Key Terms

SOP
Production Planning
MRP

PART V — Garment Factory की संरचना
अध्याय 13

Factory Layout और Department Structure

Factory Layout और Department Structure

Factory design — production efficiency का physical foundation

13.1 — Factory Layout: Physical Design matters

Factory Layout = Machines, departments, workstations और aisles का physical arrangement।

Poor Layout के consequences:

Material excessive travel: Fabric store से cutting section 100 feet। Cutting से sewing 150 feet। अनावश्यक movement = time waste।

Supervision difficult: Supervisor 3 rooms में workers देखने दौड़ता रहता है।

Safety hazards: Narrow aisles, blocked exits।

WIP piling: कुछ stations पर material stack, कुछ पर कुछ नहीं।

Communication poor: Departments अलग-अलग areas में — coordination suffer करती है।

Good Layout के benefits:

Minimum material travel। Smooth production flow। Easy supervision। Safety compliance। Good communication।

Layout Design Principles:

Principle 1 — Process Sequence: Layout को production sequence follow करनी चाहिए।

Fabric Store → Inspection → Cutting → Sewing → Finishing → QC → Packing → Dispatch

Principle 2 — Minimum Distance: हर department adjacent department के near।

Principle 3 — Flexibility: Style change, volume change accommodate कर सके।

Principle 4 — Safety First: Fire exits clear। Aisles unobstructed। First aid accessible।

Principle 5 — Supervision Visibility: Supervisor floor से सब देख सके।

13.2 — Layout Types और उनकी Comparison

Layout Type 1: Straight-Line (U या I Shape)

Material एक end से enter, दूसरे end से exit।

I-Shape: Straight line।

U-Shape: U form।

Advantages:

• Simplest to implement।

• Easy supervision।

• Material flow clear।

• WIP visible।

Best for: Single product, consistent high volume।

Layout Type 2: Module/Cell Layout

Small self-contained units (cells) — each cell has cutting + sewing + finishing for one product line।

Advantages:

• Flexible — cells can work on different styles।

• Accountability — cell team responsible for quality।

• Fast style change।

• Less WIP।

Disadvantages:

• Requires multi-skilled workers।

• Higher investment (more machines per cell)।

Best for: Multiple styles, fast fashion, Quick Response manufacturing।

Layout Type 3: Product-wise Layout

Different areas for different product types। Men's shirts in one area, women's suits in another।

Best for: Large factories with diverse product range।

Layout Type 4: Group Technology

Similar operations grouped — all collar machines together, all pocket machines together।

Best for: Large variety, frequent style changes।

Space Allocation Guide (Approximate):

Department | Space per worker/machine

Cutting (spreading + cutting table) | 50-80 sq ft/table + 20/person

Sewing machines | 35-50 sq ft per machine (inc WIP space)

Finishing/Ironing | 20-30 sq ft per station

QC Tables | 20-25 sq ft per table

Packing | 25-35 sq ft per packer

Fabric Store | 150 sq ft per 1000m storage capacity

Finished Goods | 100 sq ft per 1500-2000 pieces

For a 25-worker factory: Approximately 1200-1500 sq ft net।

With aisles, office, toilets, utility: 1600-2000 sq ft total।

13.3 — Factory के 12 Core Departments

एक complete garment factory में ये departments होते हैं:

1. RECEIVING AREA:

Incoming material receive। Challan/invoice verify। Initial count/weight। Direct to store या inspection।

2. FABRIC STORE:

Fabric organize करना। Shade grouping। FIFO। Access controlled। Temperature/humidity maintain (if critical)।

3. TRIMS STORE:

Buttons, zippers, thread, labels, packaging। Component-wise organized। Minimum quantity alert।

4. FABRIC INSPECTION SECTION:

4-point system। Shrinkage test। Shade check। Approve/reject decision।

5. CUTTING SECTION:

Spreading tables। Cutting machines। Bundling area। Numbering station।

6. SEWING SECTION:

Production lines। Machines in operation sequence। WIP storage between operations।

7. FINISHING SECTION:

Thread trimming stations। Iron/press tables। Steam press machines।

8. WASHING (if required):

Laundry machines। Drying area। Special for denim, garment-dyed products।

9. QC SECTION:

Inline QC tables (in sewing section)। End-line QC tables। Final inspection tables। AQL sampling area।

10. EMBROIDERY/PRINTING (if applicable):

Embroidery machines। Screen printing facility। Digital print area।

11. PACKING SECTION:

Folding area। Tagging station। Poly bag sealing। Carton packing।

12. FINISHED GOODS STORE/DISPATCH:

Organized by style/order। Dispatch planning। Loading area।

Support Functions:

Mechanic Workshop: Machine repair, parts storage।

Boiler/Utility Room: Steam, electricity, compressed air।

Office: Admin, accounts, sample room, meetings।

Welfare: Canteen, toilets, first aid, rest area।

13.4 — Factory Documentation System

"If it's not documented, it didn't happen."

Core Documents every factory needs:

PRODUCTION DOCUMENTS:

Job Card/Work Order: Order details, style, quantity, delivery, special instructions।

Operation Bulletin: Operations list, machine, SMV, quality points।

Production Plan: Schedule से delivery तक।

Daily Production Report: Target vs Actual, by line/section।

WIP Report: Current WIP status by stage।

QUALITY DOCUMENTS:

Inspection Checklist: Per style, per buyer spec।

QC Report: Daily defects, DHU, actions।

Defect Log: Each defect recorded।

AQL Results: Lot inspection records।

Buyer Approval Records: PP sample approvals।

MATERIAL DOCUMENTS:

GRN (Goods Receipt Note): Every material received।

Fabric Inspection Report: 4-point results।

Issue Slip: Material issued to production।

Return Slip: Excess material returned।

Inventory Register: Current stock।

HR DOCUMENTS:

Attendance Register: Daily।

Skill Assessment Sheet: Each worker।

Training Record: What, when, by whom।

Leave Register: PL, SL, CL।

Wage Register: Monthly wages।

COMPLIANCE DOCUMENTS:

Factory License।

Fire Safety Certificate।

ESI/PF registration और returns।

Labour welfare compliance records।

Audit records (social, quality)।

FINANCIAL DOCUMENTS:

Order-wise costing sheet।

Invoice (outgoing)।

Payment record।

Monthly P&L।

Document Storage:

Physical: Binder files, year-wise organized। 3-5 years minimum retention।

Digital: Scanned copies. Cloud backup। Excel summaries।

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

✅ अध्याय सारांश
Good factory layout = smooth production flow, easy supervision, safety. Straight-line और module layouts most common. 12 core departments. Documentation system = professional credibility + traceability + compliance.
